<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Nothing speculative.**\n\n- No features beyond what was asked.\n- No abstractions for single-use code.\n- No \"flexibility\" or \"configurability\" that wasn't requested.\n- No error handling for impossible scenarios.\n- If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.\n\nAsk yourself: \"Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?\" If yes, simplify.\n\n## 3. Surgical Changes\n\n**Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**\n\nWhen editing existing code:\n\n- Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n- Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n- Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n- If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n\nWhen your changes create orphans:\n\n- Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n- Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n\nThe test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n\n## 4. Goal-Driven Execution\n\n**Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**\n\nTransform tasks into verifiable goals:\n\n- \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n- \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n- \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n\nFor multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n\n```\n1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n```\n\nStrong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n\n---\n\n**These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.",

<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

17: )
18: 
19: func (c *CLab) Save(
20: 	ctx context.Context,
21: 	options ...SaveOption,
22: ) error {
23: 	opts := NewSaveOptions()
24: 	for _, opt := range options {
25: 		opt(opts)
26: 	}
27: 
28: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
29: 		return c.saveWithLabRuntime(ctx, opts)
30: 	}
31: 
32: 	if opts.copyDst != "" {
33: 		resolvedDst, err := c.resolveCopyOutDst(opts.copyDst)
34: 		if err != nil {
35: 			return err
36: 		}
37: 		opts.copyDst = resolvedDst
38: 	}
39: 
40: 	var wg sync.WaitGroup
41: 	errs := make([]error, 0, len(c.Nodes))
42: 
43: 	for _, node := range c.Nodes {
44: 		errs = append(errs, nil)
45: 		i := len(errs) - 1
46: 
47: 		wg.Add(1)
48: 		go func(node clabnodes.Node) {
49: 			defer wg.Done()
50: 
51: 			result, err := node.SaveConfig(ctx)
52: 			if err != nil {
53: 				errs[i] = fmt.Errorf("node %q save failed: %w", node.GetShortName(), err)
54: 				return
55: 			}
56: 
57: 			if opts.copyDst == "" {
58: 				return
59: 			}
60: 
61: 			if err := c.copySavedConfig(ctx, result, node, opts.copyDst); err != nil {
62: 				errs[i] = fmt.Errorf("node %q save copy failed: %w", node.GetShortName(), err)
63: 			}
64: 		}(node)
65: 	}
66: 
67: 	wg.Wait()
68: 
69: 	return errors.Join(errs...)
70: }
71: 
72: func (c *CLab) resolveCopyOutDst(dst string) (string, error) {
73: 	baseDir, err := os.Getwd()
74: 	if err != nil || baseDir == "" {
75: 		baseDir = c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir()
76: 	}
77: 	if baseDir == "" {
78: 		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to resolve save dst: current working directory is empty")
79: 	}
80: 
81: 	resolvedDst := clabutils.ResolvePath(dst, baseDir)
82: 	labDir := c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()
83: 	labDirName := filepath.Base(labDir)
84: 	if labDirName == "" || labDirName == "." {
85: 		if c.Config != nil && c.Config.Name != "" {
86: 			labDirName = "clab-" + c.Config.Name
87: 		} else {
88: 			return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to resolve save dst: lab directory is empty")
89: 		}
90: 	}
91: 
92: 	dstLabDir := filepath.Join(resolvedDst, labDirName)
93: 	if err := os.MkdirAll(dstLabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsDirDefault); err != nil {
94: 		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create save dst directory %q: %w", dstLabDir, err)
95: 	}
96: 
97: 	return dstLabDir, nil
98: }
99: 
100: // copySavedConfig copies the saved configuration file from the path in the SaveConfigResult
101: // to the user-specified destination directory with a timestamp in the filename.
102: // A symlink with the original filename is created (or updated) to point to the
103: // latest timestamped copy.
104: //
105: // The destination layout is:
106: //
107: //	<dstRoot>/<nodeName>/config-060102_150405.json   # timestamped copy
108: //	<dstRoot>/<nodeName>/config.json                 # symlink -> timestamped copy
109: func (c *CLab) copySavedConfig(
110: 	ctx context.Context,
111: 	result *clabnodes.SaveConfigResult,
112: 	node clabnodes.Node,
113: 	dstRoot string,
114: ) error {
115: 	if result == nil || result.ConfigPath == "" {
116: 		log.Debug("no saved config path reported, skipping copy", "node", node.GetShortName())
117: 		return nil
118: 	}
119: 
120: 	nodeCfg := node.Config()
121: 	if nodeCfg == nil {
122: 		return fmt.Errorf("node config missing")
123: 	}
124: 
125: 	nodeDstDir := filepath.Join(dstRoot, nodeCfg.ShortName)
126: 	if err := os.MkdirAll(nodeDstDir, clabconstants.PermissionsDirDefault); err != nil {
127: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create save dst node dir %q: %w", nodeDstDir, err)
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	fileName := filepath.Base(result.ConfigPath)
131: 	tsFileName := timestampedFileName(fileName, time.Now())
132: 
133: 	tsPath := filepath.Join(nodeDstDir, tsFileName)
134: 	if err := clabutils.CopyFile(ctx, result.ConfigPath, tsPath,
135: 		clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault); err != nil {
136: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to copy saved config from %q to %q: %w",
137: 			result.ConfigPath, tsPath, err)
138: 	}
139: 
140: 	// Create or update a symlink with the original filename pointing to the
141: 	// timestamped copy. Remove any existing file/symlink first.
142: 	linkPath := filepath.Join(nodeDstDir, fileName)
143: 	_ = os.Remove(linkPath)
144: 	if err := os.Symlink(tsFileName, linkPath); err != nil {
145: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create symlink %q -> %q: %w", linkPath, tsFileName, err)
146: 	}
147: 
148: 	log.Info(
149: 		"copied saved config",
150: 		"node", nodeCfg.ShortName,
151: 		"src", result.ConfigPath,
152: 		"dst", tsPath,
153: 	)
154: 
155: 	return nil
156: }
157: 
158: // timestampedFileName inserts a timestamp before the file extension.
159: // e.g. "config.json" with ts 2026-02-06 23:59:00 UTC -> "config-260206_235900.json".
160: // Files without an extension get the timestamp appended: "startup-config" ->
161: // "startup-config-260206_235900".
162: func timestampedFileName(name string, t time.Time) string {
163: 	ts := t.UTC().Format("060102_150405")
164: 	ext := filepath.Ext(name)
165: 	base := strings.TrimSuffix(name, ext)
166: 
167: 	return base + "-" + ts + ext
168: }

14: func TestSaveReturnsNodeErrors(t *testing.T) {
15: 	errBoom := errors.New("boom")
16: 
17: 	tests := []struct {
18: 		name    string
19: 		failing map[string]bool
20: 		wantErr []string // substrings the joined error must contain; nil means no error
21: 	}{

24: 			name:    "one node fails",

41: 			ctrl := gomock.NewController(t)
42: 			c := &CLab{Nodes: map[string]clabnodes.Node{}}
43: 			for _, name := range []string{"r1", "r2", "r3"} {

45: 				n.EXPECT().GetShortName().Return(name).AnyTimes()
46: 				var err error
47: 				if tt.failing[name] {
48: 					err = errBoom
49: 				}

51: 				c.Nodes[name] = n
52: 			}
53:

55: 
56: 			if tt.wantErr == nil {
57: 				if err != nil {
58: 					t.Fatalf("got error %v, want nil", err)
59: 				}
60: 				return
61: 			}
62: 			if !errors.Is(err, errBoom) {
63: 				t.Fatalf("got error %v, want it to wrap %v", err, errBoom)
64: 			}
65: 			for _, want := range tt.wantErr {
66: 				if !strings.Contains(err.Error(), want) {
67: 					t.Errorf("error %q does not contain %q", err, want)
68: 				}
69: 			}

71: 				t.Errorf("error reports %d failures, want %d: %q", got, len(tt.wantErr), err)
72: 			}
73: 		})

5: package linux

7: import (

16: 	clabnodesstate "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/nodes/state"

18: 	clabutils "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/utils"
19: )
20: 
21: const (
22: 	generateable     = true
23: 	generateIfFormat = "eth%d"
24: )
25: 
26: var kindnames = []string{"linux"}
27: 
28: // Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.
29: func Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {
30: 	generateNodeAttributes := clabnodes.NewGenerateNodeAttributes(generateable, generateIfFormat)
31: 	nrea := clabnodes.NewNodeRegistryEntryAttributes(nil, generateNodeAttributes, nil)
32: 
33: 	r.Register(kindnames, func() clabnodes.Node {
34: 		return new(linux)
35: 	}, nrea)
36: }
37: 
38: type linux struct {
39: 	clabnodes.DefaultNode
40: }
41: 
42: func (*linux) LinkApplyMode(context.Context) clabnodes.LinkApplyMode {
43: 	return clabnodes.LinkApplyModeLive
44: }
45: 
46: func (n *linux) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
47: 	// Init DefaultNode
48: 	n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)
49: 	n.Cfg = cfg
50: 
51: 	n.StopSignal = clabtypes.SIGKILL
52: 
53: 	// linux kind uses `always` as a default restart policy
54: 	// since often they run auxiliary services that might fail because
55: 	// of the wrong configuration or other reasons.
56: 	// Usually we want those services to automatically restart.
57: 	if n.Cfg.RestartPolicy == "" {
58: 		n.Cfg.RestartPolicy = "always"
59: 	}
60: 
61: 	for _, o := range opts {
62: 		o(n)
63: 	}
64: 
65: 	// make ipv6 enabled on all linux node interfaces
66: 	// but not for the nodes with host network mode, as this is not supported on gh action runners
67: 	if cfg.Sysctls != nil && n.Config().NetworkMode != "host" {
68: 		cfg.Sysctls["net.ipv6.conf.all.disable_ipv6"] = "0"
69: 	}
70: 
71: 	return nil
72: }
73: 
74: func (n *linux) Deploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.DeployParams) error {
75: 	// Set the "CLAB_INTFS" variable to the number of interfaces
76: 	// Which is required by vrnetlab to determine if all configured interfaces are present
77: 	// such that the internal VM can be started with these interfaces assigned.
78: 	n.Config().Env[clabconstants.ClabEnvIntfs] = strconv.Itoa(len(n.EndpointsWithParked()))
79: 
80: 	cID, err := n.Runtime.CreateContainer(ctx, n.Cfg)
81: 	if err != nil {
82: 		return err
83: 	}
84: 	_, err = n.Runtime.StartContainer(ctx, cID, n)
85: 
86: 	n.SetState(clabnodesstate.Deployed)
87: 
88: 	return err
89: }
90: 
91: func (n *linux) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {
92: 	log.Debugf("Running postdeploy actions for Linux '%s' node", n.Cfg.ShortName)
93: 
94: 	n.disableEth0TxOffload(ctx)
95: 
96: 	return nil
97: }
98: 
99: // Start re-applies the eth0 TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime recreates
100: // the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.
101: // See disableEth0TxOffload for why TX offload must stay disabled on eth0.
102: func (n *linux) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
103: 	if err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {
104: 		return err
105: 	}
106: 
107: 	// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix
108: 	// for them as well, as there is no container netns to adjust.
109: 	if n.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {
110: 		return nil
111: 	}
112: 
113: 	n.disableEth0TxOffload(ctx)
114: 
115: 	return nil
116: }
117: 
118: // disableEth0TxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the container's eth0 interface.
119: // Frames crossing the veth with an offloaded (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum break consumers
120: // that bridge or capture traffic on that interface, so the setting is (re-)applied on
121: // deploy and after every stop/start cycle, when the runtime recreates the mgmt veth.
122: //
123: // Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,
124: // and toggling its offload settings would alter the host networking state.
125: func (n *linux) disableEth0TxOffload(ctx context.Context) {
126: 	if n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "host" {
127: 		return
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.NSEthtoolTXOff(n.GetShortName(), "eth0"))
131: 	if err != nil {
132: 		log.Error(err)
133: 	}
134: }
135: 
136: func (n *linux) GetImages(_ context.Context) map[string]string {
137: 	images := make(map[string]string)
138: 	images[clabnodes.ImageKey] = n.Cfg.Image
139: 
140: 	return images
141: }
142: 
143: // CheckInterfaceName allows any interface name for linux nodes, but checks
144: // if eth0 is only used with network-mode=none.
145: func (n *linux) CheckInterfaceName() error {
146: 	nm := strings.ToLower(n.Cfg.NetworkMode)
147: 	for _, e := range n.Endpoints {
148: 		if e.GetIfaceName() == "eth0" && nm != "none" {
149: 			return fmt.Errorf(
150: 				"eth0 interface name is not allowed for %s node when network mode is not set to none",
151: 				n.Cfg.ShortName,
152: 			)
153: 		}
154: 	}
155: 	return nil
156: }

One nuance: `errs[i]` — the goroutine writes to errs[i] while the main goroutine doesn't touch it. That's safe.

1177: func (d *DefaultNode) ParkEndpoints(ctx context.Context) error {
1178: 	parkPath, err := clabutils.CreateOrGetNamedNetNS(d.parkingNetNSName())

1180: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create parking netns for node %q: %w", d.Cfg.ShortName, err)

1194: 	}

1198: 		if _, restoreErr := parkingNode.RestoreTo(ctx, d); restoreErr != nil {
1199: 			// Keep the parking netns and its endpoints so a later restore can recover them.
1200: 			d.parkingNode = parkingNode
1201: 			return errors.Join(repointErr, restoreErr)
1202: 		}

1204: 		if cleanupErr := d.cleanupParkingNetNS(); cleanupErr != nil {
1205: 			return errors.Join(repointErr, cleanupErr)
1206: 		}
1207: 		return repointErr
1208: 	}
1209: 	d.parkingNode = parkingNode
1210: 
1211: 	return nil
1212: }

Reasoning
Now I want to check where else in the codebase `n.Endpoints` is used, where the number of interfaces matters. Notably, `iol.ensureNumSlotsEnv` and `GenInterfaceConfig` now use `EndpointsWithParked`. But other places? Let me check the callers and whether `EndpointsWithParked` should be part of the `Node` interface (since these calls are on concrete types or embedded DefaultNode).

Key questions:
1. Is `EndpointsWithParked` on the `Node` interface? linux and iol embed DefaultNode; iol's method calls `n.EndpointsWithParked()` — promoted from DefaultNode. Fine.
2. What about the `iol` kind: during a recreate flow, are parked endpoints restored into the container before Deploy? The CLAB_INTFS change ensures vrnetlab waits for parked ifaces too. Let me trace the flow: where is Deploy called with parked endpoints? The context: "parked endpoints" exist when a node is being recreated (config changes) — the container is removed, endpoints parked in a netns, then re-deployed. During Deploy, endpoints are not yet adopted back? Let me trace.

Let me look at how Deploy is invoked in the recreate flow, e.g. core/topology_reconcile.go. Also check where Endpoints get re-adopted after redeploy — if the endpoints are re-adopted before Deploy, the parkingNode would have been cleared, and EndpointsWithParked == Endpoints. If endpoints are restored after container creation, then CLAB_INTFS would be wrong without this change.

Let me check the reconcile flow.
Let me trace the parking/recreate flow to understand when parked endpoints exist during `Deploy`.

1261: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q container %q not found", cfg.ShortName, cfg.LongName)
1262: 	}
1263: 
1264: 	if err := d.OverwriteNode.PreStop(ctx); err != nil {
1265: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q pre-stop hook failed: %w", cfg.ShortName, err)
1266: 	}
1267: 
1268: 	if err := d.ParkEndpoints(ctx); err != nil {
1269: 		return err
1270: 	}
1271: 
1272: 	if err := d.Runtime.StopContainer(ctx, cfg.LongName, d.StopSignal); err != nil {
1273: 		// Docker/podman may return an error while the container is already stopped.
1274: 		// if ctr is already stopped, this is OK
1275: 		if d.OverwriteNode.GetContainerStatus(ctx) == clabruntime.Stopped {
1276: 			log.Warnf(
1277: 				"node %q stop returned error but container is stopped: %v",
1278: 				cfg.ShortName,
1279: 				err,
1280: 			)
1281: 			return nil
1282: 		}
1283: 
1284: 		if restoreErr := d.RestoreEndpoints(ctx); restoreErr != nil {
1285: 			return errors.Join(
1286: 				fmt.Errorf("node %q failed stopping container: %w", cfg.ShortName, err),
1287: 				fmt.Errorf("failed to restore endpoints: %w", restoreErr),
1288: 			)
1289: 		}
1290: 
1291: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q failed stopping container: %w", cfg.ShortName, err)
1292: 	}
1293: 
1294: 	return nil
1295: }
1296: 
1297: // Start restarts a stopped container and restores its parked dataplane interfaces.
1298: func (d *DefaultNode) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
1299: 	cfg := d.Config()
1300: 
1301: 	if d.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {
1302: 		log.Debugf("node %q skips lifecycle start", cfg.ShortName)
1303: 		return nil
1304: 	}
1305: 
1306: 	status := d.OverwriteNode.GetContainerStatus(ctx)
1307: 
1308: 	switch status {
1309: 	case clabruntime.Running:
1310: 		log.Debugf("node %q already running, skipping", cfg.ShortName)
1311: 		return nil
1312: 	case clabruntime.NotFound:
1313: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q container %q not found", cfg.ShortName, cfg.LongName)
1314: 	}
1315: 
1316: 	if _, err := d.Runtime.StartContainer(ctx, cfg.LongName, d); err != nil {
1317: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q failed starting container: %w", cfg.ShortName, err)
1318: 	}
1319: 
1320: 	if err := d.RestoreEndpoints(ctx); err != nil {
1321: 		_ = d.Runtime.StopContainer(ctx, cfg.LongName, d.StopSignal)
1322: 		return err
1323: 	}
1324: 
1325: 	// Re-run topology exec commands on lifecycle start to restore node-local interface config
1326: 	// (for example IP addresses added during deploy exec phase).
1327: 	execCollection := clabexec.NewExecCollection()
1328: 	if err := d.RunExecFromConfig(ctx, execCollection); err != nil {
1329: 		log.Errorf(
1330: 			"failed to run exec commands for node %q on lifecycle start: %v",
1331: 			cfg.ShortName,
1332: 			err,
1333: 		)
1334: 	}
1335: 	execCollection.Log()
1336: 
1337: 	return nil
1338: }

14: 
15: // lifecycleNodes resolves the requested node names without applying lifecycle policy.
16: func (c *CLab) lifecycleNodes(nodeNames []string) ([]clabnodes.Node, error) {
17: 	var nodes []clabnodes.Node
18: 
19: 	if len(nodeNames) > 0 {
20: 		for _, name := range nodeNames {
21: 			n, ok := c.Nodes[name]
22: 			if !ok {
23: 				// node filter case, where referenced node in filter is not in topo file
24: 				return nil, fmt.Errorf(
25: 					"%w: node %q is not present in the topology",
26: 					claberrors.ErrIncorrectInput,
27: 					name,
28: 				)
29: 			}
30: 
31: 			nodes = append(nodes, n)
32: 		}
33: 	} else {
34: 		for _, n := range c.Nodes {
35: 			nodes = append(nodes, n)
36: 		}
37: 	}
38: 
39: 	return nodes, nil
40: }
41: 
42: func (c *CLab) parkRecreatedNodes(ctx context.Context, plan *applyPlan) error {
43: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedStringSet(plan.parkedNodeSet) {
44: 		node, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]
45: 		if !exists {
46: 			continue
47: 		}
48: 		log.Info("Parking links for recreate", "node", nodeName)
49: 		if err := node.ParkEndpoints(ctx); err != nil {
50: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed parking endpoints for node %q: %w", nodeName, err)
51: 		}
52: 	}
53: 
54: 	return nil
55: }
56: 
57: func (c *CLab) restoreRecreatedNodes(ctx context.Context, plan *applyPlan) error {
58: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedStringSet(plan.parkedNodeSet) {
59: 		node, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]
60: 		if !exists {
61: 			continue
62: 		}
63: 		log.Info("Restoring links after recreate", "node", nodeName)
64: 		if err := node.RestoreEndpoints(ctx); err != nil {
65: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed restoring endpoints for node %q: %w", nodeName, err)
66: 		}
67: 	}
68: 
69: 	return nil
70: }
71: 
72: func (c *CLab) restartApplyNodes(
73: 	ctx context.Context,
74: 	nodeSet map[string]struct{},
75: ) error {
76: 	nodeNames, err := c.networkModeNodeOrder(sortedStringSet(nodeSet))
77: 	if err != nil {
78: 		return err
79: 	}
80: 
81: 	for _, nodeName := range nodeNames {
82: 		node := c.Nodes[nodeName]
83: 		if node == nil {
84: 			continue
85: 		}
86: 
87: 		log.Info("Restarting node after link apply", "node", nodeName)
88: 		if err := node.Stop(ctx); err != nil {
89: 			return err
90: 		}
91: 		if err := node.Start(ctx); err != nil {
92: 			return err
93: 		}
94: 		if err := c.waitNodeRunning(ctx, node); err != nil {
95: 			return err
96: 		}
97: 		if err := c.waitNodeHealthyIfAvailable(ctx, node); err != nil {
98: 			return err
99: 		}
100: 	}
101: 
102: 	return nil
103: }
104: 
105: func (c *CLab) waitNodeRunning(ctx context.Context, node clabnodes.Node) error {
106: 	deadline := time.Now().Add(c.timeout)
107: 
108: 	for {
109: 		status := node.GetContainerStatus(ctx)
110: 		if clabruntime.ContainerHasJoinableNetns(status) {
111: 			return nil
112: 		}
113: 
114: 		if time.Now().After(deadline) {
115: 			return fmt.Errorf(
116: 				"node %q did not reach running state, current state %s",
117: 				node.GetShortName(),
118: 				status,
119: 			)
120: 		}
121: 
122: 		select {
123: 		case <-ctx.Done():
124: 			return ctx.Err()
125: 		case <-time.After(time.Second):
126: 		}
127: 	}
128: }
129: 
130: func (c *CLab) waitNodeHealthyIfAvailable(ctx context.Context, node clabnodes.Node) error {
131: 	if node.Config().Healthcheck == nil {
132: 		return nil
133: 	}
134: 
135: 	deadline := time.Now().Add(c.timeout)
136: 
137: 	for {
138: 		healthy, err := node.IsHealthy(ctx)
139: 		if healthy {
140: 			return nil
141: 		}
142: 		if err != nil && strings.Contains(err.Error(), "no health information") {
143: 			return nil
144: 		}
145: 		if err != nil {
146: 			log.Debugf("error checking node %q health: %v", node.GetShortName(), err)
147: 		}
148: 
149: 		if time.Now().After(deadline) {
150: 			if err != nil {
151: 				return fmt.Errorf("node %q did not become healthy: %w", node.GetShortName(), err)
152: 			}
153: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q did not become healthy", node.GetShortName())
154: 		}
155: 
156: 		select {
157: 		case <-ctx.Done():
158: 			return ctx.Err()
159: 		case <-time.After(time.Second):
160: 		}
161: 	}
162: }

Reasoning
So the flow is: park (endpoints moved to parking node, `d.Endpoints` emptied via CaptureFrom, `d.parkingNode` set), then the container is recreated (Deploy), and during Deploy the env `CLAB_INTFS` is set from `EndpointsWithParked()` which now includes parked endpoints. After deploy, RestoreEndpoints moves them back.

Now, one important question: does the recreate flow call `Deploy` while parked? Let me check the apply flow — where recreated nodes get re-deployed. Look for where redeploy/apply deploys nodes after parking. Also check `iol.Deploy` — does iol override Deploy? iol.go calls ensureNumSlotsEnv and CreateIOLFiles; where are they called from? Let me read iol.go fully relevant parts.

Also potential issue: `EndpointsWithParked` is defined on `*DefaultNode` but not in the `Node` interface. Other node kinds that embed DefaultNode but have their own Deploy (e.g. sros, ceos, etc.) still set CLAB_INTFS via DefaultNode.Deploy presumably. The linux kind has its own Deploy that overrides. iol presumably has its own Deploy too? Let me check iol.go.

Another consideration: does `iol.ensureNumSlotsEnv` and `GenInterfaceConfig` run at a time when parked endpoints exist? GenInterfaceConfig writes the interface map file; if called before restore (i.e., during deploy while parked), the endpoints are still in the parking node — using EndpointsWithParked makes it correct. If called after restore, both give the same result. Fine.

But there's a subtlety: `GenInterfaceConfig` iterates endpoints to map linux idx -> Ethernet slot/port. Parked endpoints' iface names are the same as owned ones, so it works.

Another subtlety: in `ensureNumSlotsEnv`, `n.EndpointsWithParked()` includes parked endpoints. But what about the IOL mgmt slot? needed = n.mgmtSlot + 1 initially. Fine.

Now check: is there double-counting? If a node has both owned endpoints and a parkingNode that still holds endpoints (e.g. after a failed restore, d.parkingNode set with parked endpoints AND d.Endpoints refilled?). In the failed-restore path (RestoreTo error), RestoreTo likely left endpoints in parking node and did not adopt into d. The test covers "owned and parked split after a failed restore" — d.Endpoints has eth3 owned, parkingNode has eth1, eth2. OK.

But wait — is there a scenario where an endpoint exists BOTH in d.Endpoints and in parkingNode, leading to double counting in CLAB_INTFS? In ParkEndpoints, CaptureFrom moves endpoints from d to parking node (d.Endpoints emptied). In RestoreEndpoints, RestoreTo moves them back and d.parkingNode = nil. In the failed restore path, `d.parkingNode = parkingNode` is set in ParkEndpoints (line 1200) when RepointSymlink fails and restore fails — but then d.Endpoints would be empty (captured). Hmm, but what if RestoreTo partially restored some endpoints and failed on others? Then some endpoints may be in both? Let me read parking_node.go RestoreTo and CaptureFrom.
Let me read the parking node implementation and the iol node.

205: 	}

210: func listOwnedInterfaceNames(

214: ) ([]string, error) {

220: 			return err
221: 		}
222: 
223: 		for _, link := range links {
224: 			if !isOwnedInterface(link, knownIfaceNames) {
225: 				continue
226: 			}
227:

229: 		}
230: 
231: 		return nil
232: 	})
233: 	if err != nil {
234: 		return nil, err
235: 	}
236:

238: 
239: 	return ifaces, nil
240: }
241: 
242: func isOwnedInterface(link netlink.Link, knownIfaceNames map[string]struct{}) bool {
243: 	name := link.Attrs().Name
244: 	switch name {
245: 	case "lo", "eth0":
246: 		return false
247: 	}
248: 
249: 	if _, known := knownIfaceNames[name]; !known && !hasOwnershipAltName(link) {
250: 		return false
251: 	}
252: 
253: 	return true
254: }

177: // parseMgmtIntf parses the mgmt-intf kind-specific config key and normalizes it to
178: // Ethernet<slot>/<port>;
179: // empty means the default Ethernet0/0.
180: func (n *iol) parseMgmtIntf() error {
181: 	if v := n.kindSpecificCfg().MgmtIntf; v != "" {
182: 		captureGroups, err := clabutils.GetRegexpCaptureGroups(CapturingIntfRegexp, v)
183: 		if err != nil {
184: 			return fmt.Errorf("invalid mgmt-intf %q: %w\n%s", v, err, IntfHelpMsg)
185: 		}
186: 
187: 		n.mgmtSlot, _ = strconv.Atoi(captureGroups["slot"])
188: 		n.mgmtPort, _ = strconv.Atoi(captureGroups["port"])
189: 
190: 		if n.mgmtSlot > 9 || n.mgmtPort > 3 {
191: 			return fmt.Errorf(
192: 				"invalid mgmt-intf %q: slot must be 0-9 and port must be 0-3", v)
193: 		}
194: 	}
195: 
196: 	n.mgmtIntf = fmt.Sprintf("Ethernet%d/%d", n.mgmtSlot, n.mgmtPort)
197: 	n.mgmtLinuxIdx = n.mgmtSlot*4 + n.mgmtPort
198: 
199: 	return nil
200: }

202: // swapMgmtIdx swaps linux interface index 0 with the management interface's index,
203: // so Ethernet0/0 takes the index a relocated management interface vacated.
204: func (n *iol) swapMgmtIdx(idx int) int {
205: 	switch idx {
206: 	case 0:
207: 		return n.mgmtLinuxIdx
208: 	case n.mgmtLinuxIdx:
209: 		return 0
210: 	}
211: 
212: 	return idx
213: }
214: 
215: // iolPortForLinuxIdx returns the IOL slot/port backed by linux interface index idx.
216: func (n *iol) iolPortForLinuxIdx(idx int) (slot, port int) {
217: 	mapped := n.swapMgmtIdx(idx)
218: 	return mapped / 4, mapped % 4
219: }

221: // ensureNumSlotsEnv exports CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS when the management interface is
222: // relocated, so that its slot exists even when no link lands in it.
223: func (n *iol) ensureNumSlotsEnv() {
224: 	if n.mgmtLinuxIdx == 0 || n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] != "" {
225: 		return
226: 	}
227: 
228: 	intfIdxRegexp := regexp.MustCompile(`\d+`)
229: 	needed := n.mgmtSlot + 1
230: 
231: 	for _, ep := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {
232: 		x, _ := strconv.Atoi(intfIdxRegexp.FindString(ep.GetIfaceName()))
233: 		if slot, _ := n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x); slot+1 > needed {
234: 			needed = slot + 1
235: 		}
236: 	}
237: 
238: 	n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] = strconv.Itoa(needed)
239: }

260: 	n.ensureNumSlotsEnv()

262: 	return n.CreateIOLFiles(ctx)
263: }
264: 
265: func (n *iol) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {
266: 	log.Infof("Running postdeploy actions for Cisco IOL '%s' node", n.Cfg.ShortName)
267: 
268: 	n.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)
269: 
270: 	if err := n.GenBootConfig(ctx); err != nil {
271: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to generate boot config: %w", err)
272: 	}
273: 
274: 	// Must update mgmt IP if not first boot, unless the node boots without a baseline config
275: 	if !n.firstBoot && !n.bootstrapNone {
276: 		return n.UpdateMgmtIntf(ctx)
277: 	}
278: 
279: 	return nil
280: }
281: 
282: // Start re-applies the mgmt veth TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime
283: // recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.
284: // See disableMgmtTxOffload for why IOL cannot function with it enabled.
285: func (n *iol) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
286: 	if err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {
287: 		return err
288: 	}
289: 
290: 	// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix
291: 	// for them as well, as there is no container mgmt veth to adjust.
292: 	if n.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {
293: 		return nil
294: 	}
295: 
296: 	n.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)
297: 
298: 	return nil
299: }
300: 
301: // disableMgmtTxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the host NS veth for the mgmt interface.
302: // iouyap bridges IOL interfaces over packet sockets; frames carrying an offloaded
303: // (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum reach the IOL process with an incomplete checksum, which its
304: // TCP/IP stack drops, breaking TCP/UDP connectivity.
305: //
306: // Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,
307: // and there is no clab-created mgmt veth to adjust.
308: func (n *iol) disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx context.Context) {
309: 	if n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "host" {
310: 		return
311: 	}
312: 
313: 	if n.Runtime.Mgmt().Driver == clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {
314: 		return
315: 	}
316: 
317: 	var peerIfIndex int
318: 	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.VethPeerIndex("eth0", &peerIfIndex))
319: 	if err != nil {
320: 		log.Warn("Failed to get veth peer index for IOL mgmt interface",
321: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName,
322: 			"error", err)
323: 		return
324: 	}
325: 
326: 	if err := clabutils.DisableTxOffloadByIndex(peerIfIndex); err != nil {
327: 		log.Warn("Failed to disable TX checksum offload on IOL mgmt host veth",
328: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName,
329: 			"error", err)
330: 	}
331: }
332: 
333: func (n *iol) CreateIOLFiles(ctx context.Context) error {
334: 	// If NVRAM already exists, don't need to create
335: 	// otherwise saved configs in NVRAM are overwritten.
336: 	if !clabutils.FileExists(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile)) {
337: 		// create nvram file
338: 		clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile), "")
339: 		n.firstBoot = true
340: 	}
341: 	// create these files so the bind monut doesn't automatically
342: 	// make folders.
343: 	if bootCfg := path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "boot_config.txt"); !clabutils.FileExists(bootCfg) {
344: 		clabutils.CreateFile(bootCfg, "")
345: 	}
346: 
347: 	return n.GenInterfaceConfig(ctx)
348: }
349: 
350: // Generate interfaces configuration for IOL (and iouyap/netmap).
351: func (n *iol) GenInterfaceConfig(_ context.Context) error {
352: 	// add 'boilerplate' to NETMAP and iouyap.ini for the management port
353: 	iouyapData := fmt.Sprintf(
354: 		"[default]\nbase_port = 49000\nnetmap = /iol/NETMAP\n[513:%d/%d]\neth_dev = eth0\n",
355: 		n.mgmtSlot,
356: 		n.mgmtPort,
357: 	)
358: 	netmapdata := fmt.Sprintf(
359: 		"%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\n",
360: 		n.Pid,
361: 		n.mgmtSlot,
362: 		n.mgmtPort,
363: 		n.mgmtSlot,
364: 		n.mgmtPort,
365: 	)
366: 
367: 	slot, port := 0, 0
368: 
369: 	// Regexp to pull number out of linux'ethX' interface naming
370: 	IntfRegExpr := regexp.MustCompile(`\d+`)
371: 
372: 	for _, intf := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {
373: 		// get numeric interface number and cast to int
374: 		x, _ := strconv.Atoi(IntfRegExpr.FindString(intf.GetIfaceName()))
375: 
376: 		// Interface naming is Ethernet{slot}/{port}. Each slot contains max 4 ports
377: 		slot, port = n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x)
378: 
379: 		// append data to write to NETMAP and IOUYAP files
380: 		iouyapData += fmt.Sprintf("[513:%d/%d]\neth_dev = %s\n", slot, port, intf.GetIfaceName())
381: 		netmapdata += fmt.Sprintf("%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\n", n.Pid, slot, port, slot, port)
382: 
383: 		// populate template array for config
384: 		ipv4Addr := ""
385: 		ipv4Mask := ""
386: 		v4Prefix := intf.GetIPv4Addr()
387: 		ipv6Addr := ""
388: 
389: 		if v4Prefix.IsValid() {
390: 			ipv4Addr = v4Prefix.Addr().String()
391: 			ipv4Mask = clabutils.CIDRToDDN(v4Prefix.Bits())
392: 		}
393: 
394: 		if a := intf.GetIPv6Addr(); a.IsValid() {
395: 			ipv6Addr = a.String()
396: 		}
397: 
398: 		n.interfaces = append(n.interfaces,
399: 			IOLInterface{
400: 				IfaceName: intf.GetIfaceName(),
401: 				IfaceIdx:  x,
402: 				Slot:      slot,
403: 				Port:      port,
404: 				IPv4Addr:  ipv4Addr,
405: 				IPv4Mask:  ipv4Mask,
406: 				IPv6Addr:  ipv6Addr,
407: 			},
408: 		)
409: 	}

v != \"\" {\n\t\tswitch p := clabutils.ResolvePath(v, params.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir()); {\n\t\tcase strings.EqualFold(v, \"none\"):\n\t\t\tn.bootstrapNone = true\n\t\tcase clabutils.FileExists(p):\n\t\t\tn.bootstrapCfgFile = p\n\t\tdefault:\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"bootstrap-config file %q does not exist\", p)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t_, err := n.LoadOrGenerateCertificate(params.Cert, params.TopologyName)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tn.ensureNumSlotsEnv()\n\n\treturn n.CreateIOLFiles(ctx)\n}\n\nfunc (n *iol) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {\n\tlog.Infof(\"Running postdeploy actions for Cisco IOL '%s' node\", n.Cfg.ShortName)\n\n\tn.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)\n\n\tif err := n.GenBootConfig(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to generate boot config: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Must update mgmt IP if not first boot, unless the node boots without a baseline config\n\tif !n.firstBoot && !n.bootstrapNone {\n\t\treturn n.UpdateMgmtIntf(ctx)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// Start re-applies the mgmt veth TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime\n// recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.\n// See disableMgmtTxOffload for why IOL cannot function with it enabled.\nfunc (n *iol) Start(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tif err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// DefaultNode.Start no-ops for root-ns and auto-remove nodes; skip the offload fix\n\t// for them as well, as there is no container mgmt veth to adjust.\n\tif n.ShouldSkipLifecycle() {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tn.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// disableMgmtTxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the host NS veth for the mgmt interface.\n// iouyap bridges IOL interfaces over packet sockets; frames carrying an offloaded\n// (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum reach the IOL process with an incomplete checksum, which its\n// TCP/IP stack drops, breaking TCP/UDP connectivity.\n//\n// Nodes running with host network mode are skipped: their eth0 is the host's interface,\n// and there is no clab-created mgmt veth to adjust.\nfunc (n *iol) disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx context.Context) {\n\tif n.Cfg.NetworkMode == \"host\" {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif n.Runtime.Mgmt().Driver == clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tvar peerIfIndex int\n\terr := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.VethPeerIndex(\"eth0\", &peerIfIndex))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Warn(\"Failed to get veth peer index for IOL mgmt interface\",\n\t\t\t\"node\", n.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\t\"error\", err)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif err := clabutils.DisableTxOffloadByIndex(peerIfIndex); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Warn(\"Failed to disable TX checksum offload on IOL mgmt host veth\",\n\t\t\t\"node\", n.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\t\"error\", err)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (n *iol) CreateIOLFiles(ctx context.Context) error {\n\t// If NVRAM already exists, don't need to create\n\t// otherwise saved configs in NVRAM are overwritten.\n\tif !clabutils.FileExists(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile)) {\n\t\t// create nvram file\n\t\tclabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile), \"\")\n\t\tn.firstBoot = true\n\t}\n\t// create these files so the bind monut doesn't automatically\n\t// make folders.\n\tif bootCfg := path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, \"boot_config.txt\"); !clabutils.FileExists(bootCfg) {\n\t\tclabutils.CreateFile(bootCfg, \"\")\n\t}\n\n\treturn n.GenInterfaceConfig(ctx)\n}\n\n// Generate interfaces configuration for IOL (and iouyap/netmap).\nfunc (n *iol) GenInterfaceConfig(_ context.Context) error {\n\t// add 'boilerplate' to NETMAP and iouyap.ini for the management port\n\tiouyapData := fmt.Sprintf(\n\t\t\"[default]\\nbase_port = 49000\\nnetmap = /iol/NETMAP\\n[513:%d/%d]\\neth_dev = eth0\\n\",\n\t\tn.mgmtSlot,\n\t\tn.mgmtPort,\n\t)\n\tnetmapdata := fmt.Sprintf(\n\t\t\"%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\\n\",\n\t\tn.Pid,\n\t\tn.mgmtSlot,\n\t\tn.mgmtPort,\n\t\tn.mgmtSlot,\n\t\tn.mgmtPort,\n\t)\n\n\tslot, port := 0, 0\n\n\t// Regexp to pull number out of linux'ethX' interface naming\n\tIntfRegExpr := regexp.MustCompile(`\\d+`)\n\n\tfor _, intf := range n.EndpointsWithParked() {\n\t\t// get numeric interface number and cast to int\n\t\tx, _ := strconv.Atoi(IntfRegExpr.FindString(intf.GetIfaceName()))\n\n\t\t// Interface naming is Ethernet{slot}/{port}. 573: // createNodes schedules nodes creation and returns a waitgroup for all nodes
574: // with the exec collection created from the exec config of each node.
575: // The exec collection is returned to the caller to ensure that the execution log
576: // is printed after the nodes are created.
577: // Nodes interdependencies are created in this function.
578: func (c *CLab) createNodes(
579: 	ctx context.Context,
580: 	maxWorkers uint,
581: 	skipPostDeploy bool,
582: ) (*sync.WaitGroup, *clabexec.ExecCollection, chan error, error) {

585: 	}

629: // deployApplyNodes schedules starts and creates together: a new sidecar can

647: 
648: 	if maxWorkers == 0 || int(maxWorkers) > len(nodeNames) {
649: 		maxWorkers = uint(len(nodeNames))
650: 	}
651: 	if _, err := c.networkModeNodeOrder(nodeNames); err != nil {
652: 		return err
653: 	}
654: 
655: 	input := make(chan string)
656: 	errCh := make(chan error, len(nodeNames))
657: 	completed := make(map[string]*nodeDeployCompletion, len(nodeNames))
658: 	for _, name := range nodeNames {
659: 		completed[name] = &nodeDeployCompletion{done: make(chan struct{})}
660: 	}
661: 	finish := func(name string, err error) {
662: 		completed[name].err = err
663: 		close(completed[name].done)
664: 		errCh <- err
665: 	}
666: 
667: 	for range maxWorkers {
668: 		go func() {
669: 			for nodeName := range input {
670: 				if _, start := startNodeSet[nodeName]; start {
671: 					log.Info("Starting stopped node", "node", nodeName)
672: 					err := c.Nodes[nodeName].Start(ctx)
673: 					if err != nil {
674: 						err = fmt.Errorf("failed starting node %q: %w", nodeName, err)
675: 					}
676: 					finish(nodeName, err)
677: 					continue
678: 				}
679: 				log.Info("Creating node", "node", nodeName)
680: 				finish(nodeName, c.deployNode(ctx, c.Nodes[nodeName]))
681: 			}
682: 		}()
683: 	}
684: 
685: 	// Wait outside the worker pool. Targets in this operation signal completion
686: 	// directly, avoiding runtime polling and propagating failures to dependents.
687: 	var feedWg sync.WaitGroup
688: 	for _, nodeName := range nodeNames {
689: 		feedWg.Add(1)
690: 		go func(nodeName string) {
691: 			defer feedWg.Done()
692: 			if err := c.waitForNodeDeployTarget(ctx, nodeName, completed); err != nil {
693: 				finish(nodeName, err)
694: 				return
695: 			}
696: 			select {
697: 			case input <- nodeName:
698: 			case <-ctx.Done():
699: 				finish(nodeName, ctx.Err())
700: 			}
701: 		}(nodeName)
702: 	}
703: 	go func() {
704: 		feedWg.Wait()
705: 		close(input)
706: 	}()
707: 
708: 	var errs []error
709: 	for range nodeNames {
710: 		if err := <-errCh; err != nil {
711: 			errs = append(errs, err)
712: 		}
713: 	}
714: 
715: 	if len(errs) > 0 {
716: 		return errors.Join(errs...)
717: 	}
718: 
719: 	return nil

816: func (c *CLab) deployLinks(
817: 	ctx context.Context,
818: 	links []clablinks.Link,
819: 	additionalNodeNames []string,
820: ) error {
821: 	touchedNodes := make(map[string]struct{}, len(additionalNodeNames))
822: 	for _, nodeName := range additionalNodeNames {
823: 		touchedNodes[nodeName] = struct{}{}
824: 	}
825: 	for _, link := range links {
826: 		log.Info("Creating link", "link", applyLinkName(link))
827: 
828: 		for _, ep := range clablinks.RuntimeEndpoints(link) {
829: 			if err := ep.Deploy(ctx); err != nil {
830: 				return fmt.Errorf("failed deploying link %s: %w", applyLinkName(link), err)
831: 			}
832: 			touchedNodes[ep.GetNode().GetShortName()] = struct{}{}
833: 		}
834: 
835: 		if err := link.PostDeploy(ctx); err != nil {
836: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed post-deploying link %s: %w", applyLinkName(link), err)
837: 		}
838: 	}
839: 
840: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedStringSet(touchedNodes) {
841: 		node, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]
842: 		if !exists {
843: 			continue
844: 		}
845: 		if err := node.PostDeployEndpoints(ctx); err != nil {
846: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q post-deploy links: %w", nodeName, err)

71: Default credentials: `admin:admin`
72: ///
73: 
74: ## Interface naming
75: 
76: You can use [interfaces names](../topo-def-file.md#interface-naming) in the topology file like they appear in the -{{ kind_display_name }}- CLI.
77: 
78: The interface naming convention is: `Ethernet0/X` (or `e0/X`), where `X` is the port number.
79: 
80: With the default `Ethernet0/0` management interface:
81: 
82: - `e0/1` - First data-plane interface available
83: - `e0/2` - Second data-plane interface, and so on...
84: 
85: Keep in mind IOL defines interfaces in groups of 4. Every four interfaces (zero-indexed), the slot index increments by one.
86: 
87: - `e0/3` - Third data-plane interface
88: - `e1/0` - Fourth data-plane interface
89: - `e1/1` - Fifth data-plane interface
90: 
91: The example ports above would be mapped to the following Linux interfaces inside the container running -{{ kind_display_name }}-:
92: 
93: - `eth0` - management interface connected to the containerlab management network. Mapped to `Ethernet0/0`.
94: - `eth1` - First data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet0/1` interface.
95: - `eth2` - Second data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet0/2` interface
96: - `eth3` - Third data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet0/3` interface
97: - `eth4` - Fourth data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet1/0` interface
98: - `eth5` - Fifth data-plane interface. Mapped to `Ethernet1/1` interface and so on...
99: 
100: When containerlab launches -{{ kind_display_name }}-, the `Ethernet0/0` interface of the container gets assigned management IPv4 and IPv6 addresses from docker. The `Ethernet0/0` interface is in it's own management VRF so that configuration in the global context will not affect the management interface.
101: 
102: Interfaces can be defined in a non-contiguous manner in your topology file. See the example below.
103: 
104: ```yaml
105: name: my-iol-lab
106: topology:
107:   nodes:
108:     iol-1:
109:       kind: cisco_iol

(Showing lines 30-109 of 283. Use offset=110 to continue.)
</content>

Reasoning
The docs change adds a Telnet tab. The markdown tab syntax in mkdocs-material: content tabs use `/// tab | Name` ... `///`. Nested admonition `/// warning | ...` inside a tab is supported. There's a subtlety: the diff shows the existing `///` closing the bash tab at line 54, then `/// tab | Telnet` at 55 — content tabs can be chained (a `///` immediately followed by another `/// tab |` continues the tab group). That's standard. The nested `/// warning` inside the tab closes at line 66 and the tab closes at line 68. That should render fine.

One nit: "after November 2026" — today is Nov 2026 in env; fine.

Now, let me verify the docs claim about port 5000 — does iol node code expose port 5000? Let me grep iol.go for 5000/port bindings.

56: 	// ethX naming is the "raw" or "default" interface naming.

65: // Register registers the node in the NodeRegistry.
66: func Register(r *clabnodes.NodeRegistry) {
67: 	generateNodeAttributes := clabnodes.NewGenerateNodeAttributes(generateable, generateIfFormat)

84: // KindSpecificConfig is the cisco_iol kind-specific config, set as keys on the node definition.
85: type KindSpecificConfig struct {

Reasoning
Now, let me review `DefaultNode.Deploy` at head, to see the CLAB_INTFS use and the endpoint deployment order — where the endpoints are deployed relative to container creation. Note in `DefaultNode.Deploy` (line ~187-250), the CLAB_INTFS uses EndpointsWithParked. Let me read that function to see if endpoints are deployed after container creation (so at container creation time, owned+parked = total planned endpoints). Let me also double-check the interplay: are `d.Endpoints` truly empty during Deploy for a recreated node? In `ParkEndpoints` -> `parkingNode.CaptureFrom(d)`: captureCandidates iterates present interfaces in the container's netns and adopts/moves. But does `CaptureFrom` remove the endpoints from `d.Endpoints`? It moves endpoints via `moveEndpoint(ctx, ep, p)`, which presumably calls `src.ReleaseEndpoint(ep)` and `p.AdoptEndpoint(ep)`. Let me check `moveEndpoint`.

189: 	// Set the "CLAB_INTFS" variable to the number of interfaces (endpoints) a node has.

1: package core
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"context"
5: 	"fmt"
6: 	"os"
7: 	"path/filepath"
8: 	"strings"
9: 	"sync"
10: 	"time"
11: 
12: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
13: 	clabconstants "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/constants"
14: 	clabnodes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/nodes"
15: 	clabutils "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/utils"
16: )
17: 
18: func (c *CLab) Save(
19: 	ctx context.Context,
20: 	options ...SaveOption,
21: ) error {
22: 	opts := NewSaveOptions()
23: 	for _, opt := range options {
24: 		opt(opts)
25: 	}
26: 
27: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
28: 		return c.saveWithLabRuntime(ctx, opts)
29: 	}
30: 
31: 	if opts.copyDst != "" {
32: 		resolvedDst, err := c.resolveCopyOutDst(opts.copyDst)
33: 		if err != nil {
34: 			return err
35: 		}
36: 		opts.copyDst = resolvedDst
37: 	}
38: 
39: 	var wg sync.WaitGroup
40: 
41: 	wg.Add(len(c.Nodes))
42: 
43: 	for _, node := range c.Nodes {
44: 		go func(node clabnodes.Node) {
45: 			defer wg.Done()
46: 
47: 			result, err := node.SaveConfig(ctx)
48: 			if err != nil {
49: 				log.Errorf("node %q save failed: %v", node.GetShortName(), err)
50: 				return
51: 			}
52: 
53: 			if opts.copyDst == "" {
54: 				return
55: 			}
56: 
57: 			if err := c.copySavedConfig(ctx, result, node, opts.copyDst); err != nil {
58: 				log.Errorf("node %q save copy failed: %v", node.GetShortName(), err)
59: 			}
60: 		}(node)
61: 	}
62: 
63: 	wg.Wait()
64: 
65: 	return nil
66: }
67: 
68: func (c *CLab) resolveCopyOutDst(dst string) (string, error) {
69: 	baseDir, err := os.Getwd()
70: 	if err != nil || baseDir == "" {
71: 		baseDir = c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir()
72: 	}
73: 	if baseDir == "" {
74: 		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to resolve save dst: current working directory is empty")
75: 	}
76: 
77: 	resolvedDst := clabutils.ResolvePath(dst, baseDir)
78: 	labDir := c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()
79: 	labDirName := filepath.Base(labDir)
80: 	if labDirName == "" || labDirName == "." {
81: 		if c.Config != nil && c.Config.Name != "" {
82: 			labDirName = "clab-" + c.Config.Name
83: 		} else {
84: 			return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to resolve save dst: lab directory is empty")
85: 		}
86: 	}
87: 
88: 	dstLabDir := filepath.Join(resolvedDst, labDirName)
89: 	if err := os.MkdirAll(dstLabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsDirDefault); err != nil {
90: 		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to create save dst directory %q: %w", dstLabDir, err)
91: 	}
92: 
93: 	return dstLabDir, nil
94: }
95: 
96: // copySavedConfig copies the saved configuration file from the path in the SaveConfigResult
97: // to the user-specified destination directory with a timestamp in the filename.
98: // A symlink with the original filename is created (or updated) to point to the
99: // latest timestamped copy.
100: //
101: // The destination layout is:
102: //
103: //	<dstRoot>/<nodeName>/config-060102_150405.json   # timestamped copy
104: //	<dstRoot>/<nodeName>/config.json                 # symlink -> timestamped copy
105: func (c *CLab) copySavedConfig(
106: 	ctx context.Context,
107: 	result *clabnodes.SaveConfigResult,
108: 	node clabnodes.Node,
109: 	dstRoot string,
110: ) error {
111: 	if result == nil || result.ConfigPath == "" {
112: 		log.Debug("no saved config path reported, skipping copy", "node", node.GetShortName())
113: 		return nil
114: 	}
115: 
116: 	nodeCfg := node.Config()
117: 	if nodeCfg == nil {
118: 		return fmt.Errorf("node config missing")
119: 	}
120: 
121: 	nodeDstDir := filepath.Join(dstRoot, nodeCfg.ShortName)
122: 	if err := os.MkdirAll(nodeDstDir, clabconstants.PermissionsDirDefault); err != nil {
123: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create save dst node dir %q: %w", nodeDstDir, err)
124: 	}
125: 
126: 	fileName := filepath.Base(result.ConfigPath)
127: 	tsFileName := timestampedFileName(fileName, time.Now())
128: 
129: 	tsPath := filepath.Join(nodeDstDir, tsFileName)
130: 	if err := clabutils.CopyFile(ctx, result.ConfigPath, tsPath,
131: 		clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault); err != nil {
132: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to copy saved config from %q to %q: %w",
133: 			result.ConfigPath, tsPath, err)
134: 	}
135: 
136: 	// Create or update a symlink with the original filename pointing to the
137: 	// timestamped copy. Remove any existing file/symlink first.
138: 	linkPath := filepath.Join(nodeDstDir, fileName)
139: 	_ = os.Remove(linkPath)
140: 	if err := os.Symlink(tsFileName, linkPath); err != nil {
141: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create symlink %q -> %q: %w", linkPath, tsFileName, err)
142: 	}
143: 
144: 	log.Info(
145: 		"copied saved config",
146: 		"node", nodeCfg.ShortName,
147: 		"src", result.ConfigPath,
148: 		"dst", tsPath,
149: 	)
150: 
151: 	return nil
152: }
153: 
154: // timestampedFileName inserts a timestamp before the file extension.
155: // e.g. "config.json" with ts 2026-02-06 23:59:00 UTC -> "config-260206_235900.json".
156: // Files without an extension get the timestamp appended: "startup-config" ->
157: // "startup-config-260206_235900".
158: func timestampedFileName(name string, t time.Time) string {
159: 	ts := t.UTC().Format("060102_150405")
160: 	ext := filepath.Ext(name)
161: 	base := strings.TrimSuffix(name, ext)
162: 
163: 	return base + "-" + ts + ext
164: }

Reasoning
The `Save` change is solid: errors now propagate via `errors.Join`; per-iteration `wg.Add(1)` inside the loop avoids the old pre-add race pattern (though the old was also fine). One consideration: with `errs` slice, the goroutine writes to `errs[i]` while the main goroutine appends — appends happen before goroutines start (append before `go`), and after all appends, `wg.Wait()`. But is there a happens-before issue: main goroutine appends to `errs` (which may reallocate the backing array) then spawns goroutine; goroutine writes to errs[i]; main reads errs after wg.Wait(). Each element write is to a distinct element; the append itself happens-before `go` statement? The `go` statement creates a happens-before edge from everything before it to the goroutine. So each goroutine sees a stable slice header (errs is not modified after the loop). Writes to distinct elements are safe. And wg.Wait gives happens-before for the read. Correct.

Now, `errors.Join(errs...)` — order of errors in join matches node map iteration order, which is random; fine.

One behavior consideration: previously, save failures were logged but the command returned success (exit 0). Now `clab save` returns an error → exit code non-zero. That's the intended improvement (tests document it). Any callers relying on Save returning nil? cmd/save.go returns the error. That's the point of the change. Docs for `save` command — docs/cmd/save.md exists (not changed). Does the docs say "errors ignored"? Probably not. The save.md doc is unchanged; behavior change (non-zero exit on failure) is arguably desired. Not a doc issue.

Now let me check the docs/cmd/save.md to see if anything needs updating... The change makes failures fail the command; docs likely don't claim otherwise. Quick check.

Now only if missing. Impact: On redeploy, PreDeploy no longer wipes boot_config.txt; then PostDeploy's GenBootConfig rewrites it (unless skip-postdeploy). Also for the recreate flow during apply — deployNode calls PreDeploy then Deploy; PostDeploy runs later via postDeployApplyNodes. In between, container starts and reads /iol/config.txt (boot_config.txt) — which now may contain the previous boot's generated config rather than empty. Actually the container mounts boot_config.txt: IOL reads it at boot; the container starts before PostDeploy writes the file. That seems like a race, but it's pre-existing. Hmm, wait — maybe the container's entrypoint waits. Not our concern.

With skip-postdeploy: previously boot_config.txt was blanked (empty) then the container would boot with empty config (reset); now it retains the previous generated config. With that intent, only-if-missing is exactly right. Potential issue: double counting. Could an endpoint be present in BOTH d.Endpoints and parkingNode.GetEndpoints()? Scenario: ParkEndpoints called, CaptureFrom moves all tracked+present endpoints from d to p (d.Endpoints emptied via ReleaseEndpoint in moveEndpoint). Then RestoreEndpoints succeeds → d.parkingNode = nil, endpoints adopted back into d. If RestoreEndpoints partially fails: RestoreTo on error returns without adopting; but DiscoverOwnedEndpoints may have already adopted some endpoints into `original` (d) — look at DiscoverOwnedEndpoints: it releases endpoints no longer present in the netns and adopts them into `original` (line 121-128). So on partial failure, d.Endpoints may hold some endpoints while p still holds others. That's exactly the "owned and parked split" the test describes. No double counting as long as an endpoint isn't in both. In DiscoverOwnedEndpoints, `known` map handles the case where an iface exists in the parking netns; endpoints tracked by original get re-owned to p. So the split is maintained. Good.

Now, the CLAB_INTFS concern: does `len(EndpointsWithParked())` equal the number of data interfaces the container will eventually have? During recreate, endpoints parked include runtime-discovered endpoints (from DiscoverOwnedInterfaces) as well. Fine.

Now let's double-check the test in default_node_test.go for correctness of the aliasing check: `d.Endpoints = slices.Grow(d.Endpoints, 4)` — Grow on a nil slice returns a non-nil slice with cap 4, len 0. Then AdoptEndpoint appends into it. EndpointsWithParked: parkingNode != nil → slices.Clone(d.Endpoints) creates a new slice with len = len(d.Endpoints) but cap = cap(clone)? slices.Clone returns a slice with the same length but implementation may preserve capacity — Clone guarantees len same, cap unspecified (may be > len). Then `append(clone, parked...)` may or may not reallocate. If cap(clone) == len(clone) (guaranteed? No — Clone "may" have extra capacity? Actually Go docs: "slices.Clone returns a copy of the slice. The underlying elements are copied... the result has the same length"; capacity is implementation-defined but in practice clones with capacity up to... hmm, slices.Clone uses append to an empty slice with the source's length; the result capacity is not guaranteed to equal length). So `&got[0] == &d.Endpoints[0]` check: got[0] is the clone's copy — a distinct backing array from d.Endpoints always, since Clone copies. So the aliasing check on got[0] never triggers regardless. The real aliasing risk: if `cap(clone) > len(clone) + len(parked)`, append wouldn't reallocate, but got[0] would still be clone's element 0, not d.Endpoints' element 0. Actually the danger they guard: if EndpointsWithParked returned `append(d.Endpoints, parked...)` directly (no clone), and cap allows, got[0] would BE d.Endpoints[0] and the append would write into d.Endpoints' spare capacity — not corrupting len but the next append to d.Endpoints would overwrite the parked entries... well actually appending to d.Endpoints with spare capacity would just use the shared array; d.Endpoints header unchanged so its data is intact. The corruption: the returned slice shares backing array; a later ReleaseEndpoint (which does `append(d.Endpoints[:i], d.Endpoints[i+1:]...)`) would shift elements within the shared array, mutating the caller's view. The test's check catches the non-clone case only if cap(d.Endpoints) >= len+parked — which is why they Grow by 4. But since Clone copies, got[0] never aliases. Fine — the test verifies the clone happens.

OK, the test is fine.

Hmm, wait — one important check: during `deployApplyNodes`, `deployNode` → PreDeploy → Deploy: for IOL, `GenInterfaceConfig` writes NETMAP/iouyap.ini based on EndpointsWithParked — correct for parked state. But do the parked endpoints carry IPv4/IPv6 addresses (GetIPv4Addr)? Endpoint objects preserved. Fine.

But now — what about the `mgmt` interface handling in GenInterfaceConfig: the mgmt eth0 boilerplate is separate. Fine.

Another check: `iol.CheckInterfaceName` at line 610 iterates n.Endpoints — during parking, d.Endpoints is empty so validation... it's called at parse/config time, before parking. Fine.

Now, the concern about `EndpointsWithParked` being correct in `ensureNumSlotsEnv`: during PreDeploy on recreate, parked endpoints included → CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS computed correctly. Before this change it would have been just mgmtSlot+1 → bug fixed. Good, this is the point of the PR.

Now, is there any node kind whose Deploy is overridden and iterates Endpoints during PreDeploy/Deploy that should also use EndpointsWithParked? Let me check ceos, srl, xrd, sros PreDeploy/Deploy flows for endpoint enumeration.
Let me check whether other node kinds enumerate endpoints during PreDeploy/Deploy (where parked endpoints would matter), and check the `save` docs.

Reasoning
`xrd.PreDeploy` calls `genInterfacesEnv` which enumerates `n.Endpoints` — during a recreate, this would miss parked endpoints, producing an incomplete `XR_INTERFACES` env var. The PR fixed IOL and CLAB_INTFS but not xrd. Is xrd affected? xrd is a cisco XRd container (not vrnetlab, actually it's a docker-based XRd). Its XR_INTERFACES env lists all interfaces with mapping. On recreate with parked endpoints, `n.Endpoints` is empty → XR_INTERFACES empty → the recreated xrd container wouldn't have interface mappings until... they're never re-set (env set at create time). This looks like the same class of bug the PR fixes, left unfixed. Is this "introduced or materially worsened by this PR"? The PR introduces `EndpointsWithParked` and fixes some callers. Per the ponytail rule ("Bug fix = root cause... Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller"), the incomplete fix is a legit finding: sibling callers remain broken. But is xrd's recreate flow actually affected? Let me verify xrd recreation uses parking: LinkApplyMode for xrd — DefaultNode.LinkApplyMode returns recreate unless root-ns/skip-uniqueness or boxen vendor label. XRd runs as root-ns based? xrd is "xrd-control-plane" variant which uses network mode? Let me check xrd's Init for IsRootNamespaceBased. XRd control-plane kind uses `network-mode`? Let me check.

Also check sros.Deploy (line 891) — does it enumerate endpoints? sros.go:1340 `for _, e := range n.Endpoints` — in which function? Let me check.

Reasoning
sros enumerates endpoints in CheckInterfaceName (config-time, fine).

So the PR's `EndpointsWithParked` fixes CLAB_INTFS (default + linux) and iol. The xrd `genInterfacesEnv` in PreDeploy enumerates only `n.Endpoints` — during a recreate, parked endpoints are missing, so `XR_INTERFACES` env would be built incomplete (empty) when the container is created. Before this PR, was this the same? Yes — pre-existing (base also had `n.Endpoints` there, and parking existed at base? Wait — is parking new in this PR? The base is 91c2279; the diff only touches the 7 files. `parkingNode`, `ParkEndpoints`, etc. already exist at base (they're unchanged files). So parking exists at base; the bug in xrd (and possibly others) is pre-existing, and this PR fixes a subset of callers.

Per the review scope: "Focus on issues introduced or materially worsened by this PR." The incomplete fix isn't newly introduced, but the PR introduces a new helper `EndpointsWithParked` whose purpose is exactly this, and leaves sibling callers broken — the ponytail rule cited says fix the shared function once / grep every caller. This is a legitimate P3-ish finding (pre-existing gap not covered by the fix; suggests applying the new helper to `xrd.genInterfacesEnv` at minimum, and auditing other PreDeploy-time endpoint enumerations). Let me check other PreDeploy implementations that enumerate endpoints: ceos.PreDeploy (line 162) — what does it do? Let me check ceos.PreDeploy and srl.PreDeploy, sros.PreDeploy for endpoint enumeration.
